Um, well... I guess I still enjoy things sometimes. I mean, I don't want to sound like I'm completely miserable or anything. I still like watching TV when I get home, and... and I guess I still like the idea of my hobbies. 

But, um... if I'm being completely honest, I suppose I don't really get the same kick out of things like I used to. Like those neighborhood clean-ups I mentioned... I used to really look forward to them, but now it just feels like another chore. And even just tinkering with my old radio sets, which I used to love doing on weekends... I just kind of stare at them now. I don't know, I just don't have the energy for it. 

I hope that doesn't make me sound ungrateful for the good things I have. I really do try to find the bright side. Is it normal to just kind of lose interest like that?

Um, well... I really try not to let it get in the way. I don't want you to think I'm just giving up or, you know, not doing my responsibilities. I still go to work every day and get all the building cleaning done. I make sure of that.

Um, well... I really try to keep a positive attitude, I promise I do. I don't want to sound like someone who just complains all the time or doesn't appreciate what they have.

Um, well... I mean, I definitely try to find things to enjoy. I don't just sit around doing nothing all day, I promise. I still like... well, I guess I still like getting my work done. There's a satisfaction in seeing a floor all buffed and shiny, you know? 

But as far as things I really look forward to, outside of work? Um... I guess... I guess I can't really think of anything specific right now. I know I mentioned the neighborhood clean-ups before, and I used to really love doing those, but lately it's just been hard to get the energy up for them.

Um, well... I know we sort of touched on this a minute ago, and I really don't want to sound like a broken record or like I'm making a bigger deal out of things than I should. I really do still get my cleaning done at work, I promise. I don't let the building suffer.

But... I guess if you're asking about it impacting me significantly... well, like I was saying before, I'm definitely a lot slower. And I just keep double-checking my maintenance logs, second-guessing every little thing I write down. It's gotten to the point where I just... I stopped putting my name in for the supervisor track. I didn't want to take it on and then let management down, you know? 

And then outside of work, I've just been skipping the neighborhood clean-ups and even getting together with my family, which I always used to make time for. I just... I haven't had it in me. 

I hope I'm not sounding like I'm just giving up on everything. I really am trying my best to keep it together. Does... does that sound like what you mean by significantly impacting my life? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.

I guess I've been skipping the neighborhood clean-ups I used to do, and I haven't been making it to family get-togethers as much. I just feel so drained. I hope I'm not just whining.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that this heavy, flat feeling you described is similar to feeling sad or down?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah... I guess it is. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... well, eating is mostly fine, I promise. I make sure I get my meals in, so you don't have to worry about that part. I really try to keep on top of things like that. 

But, um... I guess the sleeping part is a little trickier. I really don't want to sound like I'm just complaining, because I know working overnight shifts at the building messes with everyone's schedule, but... my sleep has just been kind of messed up for a long time now. 

Usually, after I get off a shift, I'll get home and just lie there in bed. I'll be so incredibly exhausted, but my brain is just... wired, I guess? And the frustrating thing is, even when I do finally drift off and get a decent number of hours, I wake up feeling like I barely rested at all. 

I swear I try to practice good habits and get enough rest so I can do a good job at work. I hope it doesn't sound like I'm just completely failing at taking care of myself. Is... is it okay that I'm struggling with that part a bit? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the full picture without sounding like I'm making excuses.

I came in because, well, my head just doesn't seem to want to stay on task anymore. It's probably nothing major, I know you see people with much bigger problems, but... like, I'll be doing inventory at work, or trying to study for this supervisor certification I want to get, and I just keep reading the same line over and over. Or I'll walk into the supply closet and completely forget what I went in there for. Is that... does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: When did you first notice that your head couldn't stay on task?\n\nPatient: Um, well, it's been a while, honestly. Probably about three years now? I hope it's okay that I waited this long to come in... I just kept thinking it would get better on its own. It started around the time the contractor I work for switched our schedules. They moved us to these rotating evening and overnight shifts, usually at the bigger office buildings. I mean, I'm grateful to have the job, and I don't want to complain about the hours, but... yeah, that's about when I started noticing it. Does that timeline seem helpful to you?\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble completing tasks at work or in your daily routine because of these memory and focus issues?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I wouldn't say I'm completely failing at my job or anything. I still get all the cleaning done, I promise. I just... I think I'm a bit slower than I used to be. And I end up second-guessing my inventory logs a lot, which takes up extra time. I actually stopped studying for that supervisor track because of it. I just figured I wasn't cut out for it right now, you know? And outside of work... I guess I've been skipping the neighborhood clean-ups I used to do, and I haven't been making it to family get-togethers as much. I just feel so drained. I hope that doesn't make me sound lazy or unreliable. I really do try to keep up with everything. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling drained - can you tell me more about what you mean by that?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I can try to explain it better. I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is. It's just... well, most days, I feel like there's this heavy, flat feeling that just kind of follows me around. Like, even when I'm just pushing my cleaning cart down an empty hallway at night, or... or even if I'm sitting with my family and trying to act normal and smile. It's just always there, weighing me down. I try not to let it show, because I don't want to bring anyone else down, you know? But it just makes everything feel so exhausting. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not just whining.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that this heavy, flat feeling you described is similar to feeling sad or down?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah... I guess it is. I'm sorry if I wasn't very clear a second ago. But yeah, like I was just saying about that heavy feeling following me around when I'm pushing my cart, or when I'm trying to act normal with my family... I suppose it really is just feeling sad. Just a constant, down sort of feeling. \n\nI'm not crying all the time or anything dramatic like that, I promise! I don't want you to think I'm completely falling apart or can't handle things. It's just... yeah, dull and sad. Does that make sense? I hope that answers your question better.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I still enjoy things sometimes. I mean, I don't want to sound like I'm completely miserable or anything. I still like watching TV when I get home, and... and I guess I still like the idea of my hobbies. \n\nBut, um... if I'm being completely honest, I suppose I don't really get the same kick out of things like I used to. Like those neighborhood clean-ups I mentioned... I used to really look forward to them, but now it just feels like another chore. And even just tinkering with my old radio sets, which I used to love doing on weekends... I just kind of stare at them now. I don't know, I just don't have the energy for it. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound ungrateful for the good things I have. I really do try to find the bright side. Is it normal to just kind of lose interest like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy social interactions, like spending time with friends or family, as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, I still love my family, of course. I don't want you to think I'm withdrawing from them or being a bad son or brother or anything. I still go to Sunday dinners and try to be present. \n\nBut, um... I guess if I'm really thinking about it... it's kind of like with the hobbies. I'm there, and I'm smiling, but I just don't feel that same connection or... or warmth, I guess? It just feels like a lot of effort to keep up the conversation and make sure everyone thinks I'm doing okay. I usually just end up feeling really drained afterward, even though they're great people. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound terrible. I really do try to be good company. Do a lot of people feel that way around their families when they're... feeling like this?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods in the past two weeks where you felt better, or where the sad feelings lifted for a little while?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, I don't want you to think it's just doom and gloom every single second. I'm sure I've had some okay moments. Like, um, maybe if I have a good cup of coffee or someone tells a joke at work, I'll smile. I'm not just a black cloud all the time, I promise.\n\nBut, um... as far as the heavy feeling actually lifting? Like, really going away? I guess... I guess not really. Not in the last couple of weeks. It's just always sort of there in the background, even if I'm trying my best to ignore it and be positive. \n\nI hope that's an okay answer. I just... I can't really think of a time recently where I felt completely light or, you know, back to normal. Is that what you meant?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that this sad feeling is interfering with your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I really try not to let it get in the way. I don't want you to think I'm just giving up or, you know, not doing my responsibilities. I still go to work every day and get all the building cleaning done. I make sure of that.\n\nBut, um... if I'm looking at it honestly... I guess it is affecting things a little bit. I'm definitely slower than I used to be. And I find myself second-guessing my maintenance logs so much lately, just worrying I missed something or wrote it down wrong. Because of that, I actually stopped pushing for the supervisor track at work... I just didn't want to let management down if I couldn't handle the extra pressure right now. \n\nAnd, well, like I mentioned before with the neighborhood clean-ups and my family... I've actually been skipping those a lot lately, even though I used to always make time for them. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound lazy or like I'm making excuses. I really am still trying to keep up with the important stuff. Does... does that make sense? Is that the kind of thing you mean by interfering?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that this sad feeling is present nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I really try to keep a positive attitude, I promise I do. I don't want to sound like someone who just complains all the time or doesn't appreciate what they have. \n\nBut, um... yeah, I guess if I'm being completely honest about it, it is there most days. It's just... it's like there's this heavy, flat feeling that sort of follows me around everywhere. Even when I'm just pushing my cleaning cart down an empty hallway at work, or, you know, sitting with my family and trying to act normal for them... it's just kind of sitting there with me. \n\nI really do try to shake it off and focus on the good things, though. I hope I'm explaining this right... is it common for people to feel it almost every day like that?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and look forward to?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, I definitely try to find things to enjoy. I don't just sit around doing nothing all day, I promise. I still like... well, I guess I still like getting my work done. There's a satisfaction in seeing a floor all buffed and shiny, you know? \n\nBut as far as things I really look forward to, outside of work? Um... I guess... I guess I can't really think of anything specific right now. I know I mentioned the neighborhood clean-ups before, and I used to really love doing those, but lately it's just been hard to get the energy up for them. \n\nI don't want you to think I'm completely miserable or that I don't care about anything anymore. I really do try to stay engaged with my family and everything. It's just... maybe things don't spark that same excitement they used to? I hope that's an okay answer. Is it normal to not really have a lot of hobbies you're super excited about right now?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that this sad feeling is significantly impacting your ability to function in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I know we sort of touched on this a minute ago, and I really don't want to sound like a broken record or like I'm making a bigger deal out of things than I should. I really do still get my cleaning done at work, I promise. I don't let the building suffer.\n\nBut... I guess if you're asking about it impacting me significantly... well, like I was saying before, I'm definitely a lot slower. And I just keep double-checking my maintenance logs, second-guessing every little thing I write down. It's gotten to the point where I just... I stopped putting my name in for the supervisor track. I didn't want to take it on and then let management down, you know? \n\nAnd then outside of work, I've just been skipping the neighborhood clean-ups and even getting together with my family, which I always used to make time for. I just... I haven't had it in me. \n\nI hope I'm not sounding like I'm just giving up on everything. I really am trying my best to keep it together. Does... does that sound like what you mean by significantly impacting my life? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that this sad feeling is worse most of the time, or is it about the same all the time?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I really try not to focus on it being worse or better, to be honest. I don't want to dwell on the negative too much, you know? I really try to just keep moving forward.\n\nBut, um... if I have to really think about it... I guess I'd say it's mostly just... the same. It's just sort of this constant, low-level thing. It doesn't usually spike into something unbearable, I promise. It's just kind of... always there in the background, like a hum you can't quite turn off. \n\nI hope that makes sense. Is it better if it's just the same all the time instead of getting worse? I really am trying to just manage it as best I can.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I know you just asked me something similar, and I really hope I'm not giving you bad answers or being difficult. I promise I'm trying to think of something good to tell you. \n\nI guess... I mean, I really do still like making sure my sections at work are clean. I take pride in that, I really do. But as for, like, fun stuff or hobbies... um, I don't know if I can think of anything right now that I'm just really, really enjoying. I know I should have something, and I'm sorry if that's not what you're looking for. \n\nI don't want you to think I'm just a totally negative person. I still try to smile and be polite to everyone in the building. It's just... I guess the enjoyment part is just a little hard to find lately. Is it okay that I don't really have a good answer for that right now?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that this sad feeling is making it hard for you to take care of your basic needs, like eating or sleeping?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, eating is mostly fine, I promise. I make sure I get my meals in, so you don't have to worry about that part. I really try to keep on top of things like that. \n\nBut, um... I guess the sleeping part is a little trickier. I really don't want to sound like I'm just complaining, because I know working overnight shifts at the building messes with everyone's schedule, but... my sleep has just been kind of messed up for a long time now. \n\nUsually, after I get off a shift, I'll get home and just lie there in bed. I'll be so incredibly exhausted, but my brain is just... wired, I guess? And the frustrating thing is, even when I do finally drift off and get a decent number of hours, I wake up feeling like I barely rested at all. \n\nI swear I try to practice good habits and get enough rest so I can do a good job at work. I hope it doesn't sound like I'm just completely failing at taking care of myself. Is... is it okay that I'm struggling with that part a bit? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the full picture without sounding like I'm making excuses.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but no longer find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm really trying to think, because I want to give you a good answer. But, honestly... I can't really come up with anything specific. I hope that's alright. \n\nI don't really have a lot of big hobbies that I used to do and just stopped. I've mostly just kept to my same routine for a really long time, you know? Just going to the building for my shifts and coming home. I promise I'm not trying to be difficult or hide anything from you. It's just... things have kind of been this way for so long, I don't really have a time where I was doing a bunch of fun stuff to compare it to. \n\nDoes that make sense? I really hope it's okay that I don't have a better answer for you on that one. I'm trying my best to give you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to doing?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I just kind of focus on getting my work done at the building and getting through the week. \n\nI'm really sorry, I know that's probably not a very helpful answer, and I really wish I had some exciting plans or hobbies to tell you about. I promise I'm not trying to be difficult. Is it alright that I'm still coming up blank on this? Um... I don't want to make it sound like I can't do my job, because I really do. I still get all the cleaning done at the building, I promise. I haven't let that slip. 

But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest, maybe it's affecting me a little bit? Like, I'm definitely slower than I used to be. I really hope that doesn't sound too bad. I'm still working hard, I really am. Do you think that counts as interfering in a major way? I just want to give you the right answer.
